Lemma 16

(Unary Functorial Substitution Lemma)
.

Let
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
be two exponential signatures such that
\sigma_{1}\leq_{\text{u}}\sigma_{2}
.
Let
A
and
B
be formulas, such that for all
\Gamma
, if
\vdash A,\Gamma
is provable without using any cut then
\vdash B,\Gamma
is provable without using any cut.
Then we have that for all
\Gamma
, if
\vdash\overbrace{\wn_{\sigma_{1}}A,\dotsc,\wn_{\sigma_{1}}A}^{n},\Gamma
is provable without using any cut then
\vdash\overbrace{\wn_{\sigma_{2}}B,\dotsc,\wn_{\sigma_{2}}B}^{n},\Gamma
as well, with
k_{i}
positive integers.

Proof.

This lemma is proven the same way as Lemma
15
.
∎

Finally we prove cut-elimination theorem
2
:

Theorem 4

(Cut Elimination)
.

Cut elimination holds for
\textnormal{{$\text{superLL}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
as soon as the 8 cut-elimination axioms of
\Cref
cutElimAxs are satisfied.

Proof.

We prove the result by induction on the couple
(t,s)
with lexicographic order, where
t
is the size of the cut formula and
s
is the sum of the sizes of the premises of the cut. We distinguish cases depending on the last rules of the premises of the cut:

•

If one of the premises does not end with a rule acting on the cut formula, we apply the induction hypothesis with the premise(s) of this rule.

•

If both last rules act on the cut formula which does not start with an exponential connective, we apply the standard reduction steps for non-exponential cuts leading to cuts involving strictly smaller cut formulas. We conclude by applying the induction hypothesis.

•

If we have an exponential cut for which the cut formula
\oc_{\sigma_{1}}A^{\perp}
is not the conclusion of a promotion rule introducing
\oc_{\sigma_{1}}
, the rule above
\oc_{\sigma_{1}}A^{\perp}
cannot be a promotion rule, and we apply the induction hypothesis to its premise(s).

•

If we have an exponential cut for which the cut formula
\oc_{\sigma_{1}}A^{\perp}
is the conclusion of an (
{\oc_{\text{g}}}
)-rule. We can apply:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 23.25493pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash A^{\perp},\wn_{\vec{\sigma_{2}}}\Delta$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\sigma_{1}\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\sigma_{2}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.40091pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to106.13318pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.5398pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt${\oc_{\text{g}}}$}}\vskip-2.4009pt\hbox{\hskip 19.8208pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\oc_{\sigma_{1}}A^{\perp},\wn_{\vec{\sigma_{2}}}\Delta$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\wn_{\sigma_{1}}A,\Gamma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.57353pt\hbox{\hskip 19.8208pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to159.87233pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 2.57352pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\text{cut}$}}\vskip-0.57352pt\hbox{\hskip 75.91844pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\wn_{\vec{\sigma_{2}}}\Delta,\Gamma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\quad\rightsquigarrow\quad\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.77222pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\wn_{\sigma_{1}}A,\Gamma$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\sigma_{1}\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\sigma_{2}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.8pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox{}\hbox to100.29842pt{\leaders{\hbox to7.96677pt{\hss\vrule width=3.98337pt,height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt\hss}}{\hfill}}\lower 2.3pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\text{Lem.}~\ref{subs1}$}}\vskip-2.8pt\hbox{\hskip 26.31068pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\wn_{\vec{\sigma_{2}}}\Delta,\Gamma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces

We have that
A
and
\Delta
are such that for every
\Gamma
such that
\vdash A,\Gamma
is provable without cuts,
\vdash\wn_{\vec{\sigma_{2}}}\Delta,\Gamma
too. Indeed,
A
and
\Delta
are such that
\vdash A^{\perp},\wn_{\vec{\sigma_{2}}}\Delta
is provable without cuts and we can apply the induction hypothesis (
\#(A)<\#(\wn_{\sigma_{1}}A)
). Therefore, we can apply Lemma
14
on
\vdash\wn_{\sigma_{1}}A,\Gamma
and obtain that
\vdash\wn_{\vec{\sigma_{2}}}\Delta,\Gamma
is provable without cut.

•

If we have an exponential cut for which the cut formula
\oc_{\sigma_{1}}A^{\perp}
is the conclusion of an (
{\oc_{\text{f}}}
)-rule. We can apply:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 23.59521pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash A^{\perp},\Delta$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\sigma_{1}\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\sigma_{2}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-1.72035pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to92.86581pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 2.22035pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt${\oc_{\text{f}}}$}}\vskip-1.72035pt\hbox{\hskip 13.18712pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\oc_{\sigma_{1}}A^{\perp},\wn_{\vec{\sigma_{2}}}\Delta$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\wn_{\sigma_{1}}A,\Gamma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.57353pt\hbox{\hskip 13.18712pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to151.7317pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 2.57352pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\text{cut}$}}\vskip-0.57352pt\hbox{\hskip 65.21445pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\wn_{\vec{\sigma_{2}}}\Delta,\Gamma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\quad\rightsquigarrow\quad\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.77222pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\wn_{\sigma_{1}}A,\Gamma$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\sigma_{1}\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\sigma_{2}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.8pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox{}\hbox to98.79147pt{\leaders{\hbox to7.96677pt{\hss\vrule width=3.98337pt,height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt\hss}}{\hfill}}\lower 2.3pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\text{Lem.}~\ref{subs2}$}}\vskip-2.8pt\hbox{\hskip 25.55722pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\wn_{\vec{\sigma_{2}}}\Delta,\Gamma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces

We have that
A
and
\Delta
are such that for every
\Gamma
such that
\vdash A,\Gamma
is provable without cuts,
\vdash\Delta,\Gamma
too. Indeed,
A
and
\Delta
are such that
\vdash A^{\perp},\Delta
is provable without cuts and we can apply the induction hypothesis. Therefore, we can apply
\Cref
subs2 on
\vdash\wn_{\sigma_{1}}A,\Gamma
and obtain that
\vdash\wn_{\vec{\sigma_{2}}}\Delta,\Gamma
is provable without cut.

•

If we have an exponential cut for which the cut formula
\oc_{\sigma_{1}}A^{\perp}
is the conclusion of an (
{\oc_{\text{u}}}
)-rule, this case is treated in the exact same way as (
{\oc_{\text{f}}}
), using
\Cref
subs3.

∎

B.3
Details on
ELL
as instance of
superLL

Elementary Linear Logic.

Elementary Linear Logic (
ELL
)
[
16
,
12
]
is a variant of
LL
where we remove (
\wn_{\text{d}}
) and
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
and add the functorial promotion:

\vdash A,\Gamma

{\oc_{\text{f}}}

\vdash\oc A,\wn\Gamma

It is the
\textnormal{{$\text{superLL}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
system with
\mathcal{E}=\{\bullet\}
, defined by
\bullet(\wn_{\text{c}_{2}})=\bullet(\wn_{\text{m}_{0}})=\text{true}
(and
(\bullet)(r)=\text{false}
otherwise),
{\leq_{\text{g}}}={\leq_{\text{u}}}=\emptyset
and
\bullet\leq_{\text{f}}\bullet
.
This
\textnormal{{$\text{superLL}$}}(\mathcal{E},,{\leq_{\text{g}}},{\leq_{\text{f}}},{\leq_{\text{u}}})
instance is
ELL
and satisfies the cut-elimination axioms and the expansion axiom:

•

The rule (
\wn_{\text{m}_{0}}
) is the weakening rule (
\wn_{\text{w}}
), (
\wn_{\text{c}_{2}}
) is the contraction rule (
\wn_{\text{c}}
), and we can always apply promotion (
{\oc_{\text{f}}}
) as
{\leq_{\text{f}}}
is the plain relation on
\mathcal{E}
:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 21.27965pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash A,\Gamma$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 10.37851pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to28.75702pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\bullet\leq_{\text{f}}\bullet$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-1.72035pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to78.29405pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 2.22035pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt${\oc_{\text{f}}}$}}\vskip-1.72035pt\hbox{\hskip 13.25815pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\oc_{\bullet}A,\wn_{\bullet}\Gamma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\qquad\leftrightsquigarrow\qquad\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.07132pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.37502pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash A,\Gamma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.40091pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to43.83328pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.5398pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\oc_{f}$}}\vskip-2.4009pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash\oc A,\wn A$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces

We have that (
{\oc_{\text{g}}}
) is a restriction of (
{\oc_{\text{f}}}
) in
ELL
and (
{\oc_{\text{u}}}
) is non-existent.

•

Moreover, the cut-elimination axioms are satisfied.
As
\mathcal{E}
is a singleton, axioms
(\mathsf{Ax^{g}_{m})}
,
(\mathsf{Ax^{fu}_{m})}
,
(\mathsf{Ax_{c})}
,
(\mathsf{Ax_{trans})}
,
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{gs})}
,
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{fu})}
,
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{us})}
hold. Axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{fg})}
is vacuously satisfied.

•

The expansion axiom is satisfied since
\leq_{\text{f}}
is reflexive.

Appendix C
Details on
\Cref
section:musuperll

C.1
Details on
\mu\text{LL}_{\Box}^{\infty}
as an instance of
\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}

We show here in details how the system
\mu\text{LL}_{\Box}^{\infty}
is an instance of super exponentials.

\mu\text{LL}_{\Box}^{\infty}
coincides with the system
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
such that:

•

The set of signatures contains two elements
\mathcal{E}:=\{\bullet,\star\}
.

•

\wn_{\text{c}_{2}}(\bullet)=\wn_{\text{c}_{2}}(\star)=\text{true}

•

\wn_{\text{m}_{1}}(\bullet)=\text{true}
,

•

\wn_{\text{m}_{0}}(\bullet)=\wn_{\text{m}_{0}}(\star)=\text{true}
,

•

all the other elements have value
false
for both signatures.

•

\bullet\leq_{\text{g}}\bullet
;
\bullet\leq_{\text{g}}\star
,
\star\leq_{\text{f}}\star
, and all other couples for the three relations
\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}}
and
\leq_{\text{u}}
being false.

This system is
\mu\text{LL}_{\Box}^{\infty}
when taking:

\wn_{\bullet}:=\wn,\quad\oc_{\bullet}:=\oc,\quad\wn_{\star}:=\lozenge\quad\text{and}\quad\oc_{\star}:=\Box.

We can indeed check that the system satisfies the cut-elimination axioms of
\Cref
cutElimAxs:

•

Hypotheses of axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{c})}
are ony true for
i=2
in two cases: for
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}=\bullet
, in that case
\bar{\sigma}(\wn_{\text{c}_{2}}
is true because
\sigma(\wn_{\text{c}_{2}})
is; or for
\sigma=\bullet
and
\sigma^{\prime}=\star
, in that case the axiom is satisfied as
\sigma^{\prime}(\wn_{\text{c}_{2}})
is true.

•

Hypotheses of axiom
(\mathsf{Ax^{g}_{m})}
are true for
i=0
when
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}=\bullet
, or for
\sigma=\bullet
and
\sigma^{\prime}=\star
, in both cases we have that
\bar{\sigma^{\prime}}(\wn_{\text{c}_{0}})
is true because
\sigma^{\prime}(\wn_{\text{m}_{0}})
is true.

•

Axiom
(\mathsf{Ax^{g}_{m})}
is always true for
i=1

•

Hypotheses of axiom
(\mathsf{Ax^{g}_{m})}
are not satisfied for
i>1
.

•

Hypotheses of axiom
(\mathsf{Ax^{fu}_{m})}
are satisfied only for
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}=\star
and so easily satisfied.

•

Axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{trans})}
is satisfied as
\leq_{\text{g}}
and
\leq_{\text{f}}
are transitive.

•

Hypotheses of axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{gs})}
are only satisfied for
\sigma=\bullet
and
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma^{\prime\prime}=\star
, and in this case the conclusion is one of the hypothesis.

•

Hypotheses of the other axioms are never fully satisfied.

Appendix D
Details on
\Cref
section:cut-elimination

D.1
Details on the justification of (
mcut
)-steps

In the following, we shall prove the lemmas justifying the mcut-reduction steps.
The following statement are identical to those found in the body of the paper but for the fact that we make explicit the side conditions on the exponential rules: in the hypotheses of the lemmas, such side-conditions are assumptions we can use in our proof while in the conclusion derivation these side-conditions are goals to be proved in order to establish that the derivation is indeed a proof in the considered
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
system.

D.1.1
Justification for step
({\text{comm}}_{\oc_{\text{g}}})
: proof of
\Cref
ocgreadinesscondition

The case
({\text{comm}}_{\oc_{\text{g}}})
covers all the case where
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
commute under the cut:

Lemma 17

(Justification for step
({\text{comm}}_{\oc_{\text{g}}})
)
.

If

\pi

\vdash A,\wn_{\vec{\tau}}\Delta

\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau}

{\oc_{\text{g}}}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\tau}}\Delta

\mathcal{C}^{\oc}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma

is a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof then

\pi

\vdash A,\wn_{\vec{\tau}}\Delta

\mathcal{C}^{\oc}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash A,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma

\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\rho}

{\oc_{\text{g}}}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma

is also a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof.

Proof.

We prove that for each sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}A^{\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{C}^{\oc}\cup\{\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\tau}}\Delta\}
, we have that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime}}
.

The
\perp\!\!\!\perp
-relation extended to sequent defines a tree on
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
. Taking
\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\tau}}\Delta
as the root, the ancestor relation of this tree is a well-founded relation. We can therefore do a proof by induction:

•

The base case is given by the condition of application of
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
in the proof.

•

For heredity, we have that there is a sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\sigma^{\prime}}({A^{\prime}}^{\perp})
of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, connected on
\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}A^{\prime}
to our sequent.
By induction hypothesis, we have that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\sigma^{\prime}
.
The rule on top of
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}A^{\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime}
is a promotion. We have two cases:

–

If it’s a
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-promotion, we can use axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{trans})}
with the application condition of the promotion, to get
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime}}
.

–

If it’s an
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
-promotion or an
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-promotion, we can use axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{gs})}
with the application condition of the promotion, to get
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime}}
.

We conclude by induction and use the inequalities to prove that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\rho}
.
∎

D.1.2
Justification for step
({\text{comm}}^{1}_{\oc_{\text{f}}})
: proof of
\Cref
ocfreadinesscondition

The case
({\text{comm}}^{1}_{\oc_{\text{f}}})
covers the case of commutation of an
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
-promotion but where only
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-rules with empty contexts appears in the hypotheses of the multi-cut.
Note that an
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
occurrence with empty context could be seen as an
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
occurrence (with empty context).

Lemma 18

(Justification for step
({\text{comm}}^{1}_{\oc_{\text{f}}})
)
.

If

\pi

\vdash A,\Delta

\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\tau}

{\oc_{\text{f}}}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\tau}}\Delta

\mathcal{C}^{\oc}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma

is a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof with
\mathcal{C}^{\oc}
such that each sequents concluded by an
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
have an empty context, then

\pi

\vdash A,\Delta

\mathcal{C}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash A,\Gamma

\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\rho}

{\oc_{\text{f}}}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma

is a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof.

Proof.

We prove that for each sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}A^{\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{C}^{{\oc_{\text{g}}}}\cup\{\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\tau}}\Delta\}
,
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\tau^{\prime}}
.

The
\perp\!\!\!\perp
-relation extended to sequent defines a tree on
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
. Taking
\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\tau}}\Delta
as the root, the ancestor relation of this tree is a well-founded relation. We can therefore do an induction proof:

•

The base case is given by the condition of application of
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
in the proof.

•

For heredity, we have that there is a sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\sigma^{\prime}}({A^{\prime}}^{\perp})
of
C^{\prime}
, connected on
\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}A^{\prime}
to our sequent.
By induction hypothesis, we have that
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\sigma^{\prime}
.
The rule on top of
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}A^{\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime}
is a promotion. We have three cases:

–

If it’s an
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-promotion, then the context is empty and the proof is easily satisfied.

–

If it’s an
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
-promotion, we can use axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{trans})}
with the application condition of the promotion to get
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\tau^{\prime}}
.

–

If it’s an
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-promotion, we can use axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{fu})}
with the application condition of the promotion to get
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\tau^{\prime}}
.

We conclude by induction and use the inequalities to prove that
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\rho}
.
∎

D.1.3
Justification for step
({\text{comm}}^{2}_{\oc_{\text{f}}})
: proof of
\Cref
ocfgreadinesscondition

We then have the following case where we commute an
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
-rule, but where there is one (at least)
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-promotion with a non-empty context in the premisses of the multicut rule:

Lemma 19

(Justification for step
({\text{comm}}^{2}_{\oc_{\text{f}}})
)
.

If

\pi

\vdash A,\Delta

\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\tau}

{\oc_{\text{f}}}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\tau}}\Delta

\mathcal{C}^{\oc}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma

is a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof with
\mathcal{C}^{{\oc_{\text{g}}}}
containing a sequent conclusion of an
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-rule with at least one formula in the context, then

\pi

\vdash A,\Delta

\vec{\tau}(\wn_{\text{m}_{1}})

\wn_{\text{m}_{1}}

\vdash A,\wn_{\vec{\tau}}\Delta

\mathcal{C}^{\oc}

\vdash A,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma

\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\rho}

{\oc_{\text{g}}}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma

is also a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof.

Proof.

We prove that for each sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}A^{\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{C}^{\oc}:=\mathcal{C}^{{\oc_{\text{g}}}}_{1}\cup\mathcal{C}^{{\oc_{\text{f}}}}_{2}\cup\mathcal{C}^{{\oc_{\text{u}}}}_{3}\cup\{\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\tau}}\Delta\}
, we have that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime}}
. Moreover, we prove that
\vec{\tau}(\wn_{\text{m}_{1}})
.
We prove that in two steps:

1.

There is a sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}A^{\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime}
, with
\Delta^{\prime}
being non-empty, which is conclusion of an
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-rule. Let’s suppose without loss of generality, that this sequent is the closest such sequent to
S:=\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\tau}}\Delta
.
The
\perp\!\!\!\perp
-relation extended to sequents defines a tree with the hypotheses of the multi-cut rule, therefore there is a path from the sequent
S
to the sequent
S^{\prime}:=\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}A^{\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime}
, of sequents
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
. We prove by induction on this path, starting from
S
and stopping one sequent before
S^{\prime}
that
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\tau^{\prime\prime}
:

•

The initialisation comes from the condition of application of
{\oc_{\text{f}}}
on
S
.

•

For the heredity, we have that the sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is cut-connected to a
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{(3)}}A^{(3)},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{(3)}}}\Delta^{(3)},\wn_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}({A^{\prime\prime}}^{\bot})
on
\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime}
, therefore
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
. We have two cases: either this sequent is the conclusion of an
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-rule and we apply axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{fu})}
, either of an
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
-rule and we apply axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{trans})}
. In each case, we have that
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
.

We conclude by induction and get a sequent
S^{\prime\prime}:=\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
cut-connected to
S^{\prime}
on the formula
\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}A^{\prime}
with
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
. From that we get that
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\sigma^{\prime}
. Moreover, we have that
\sigma^{\prime}\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime}}
. As
\Delta^{\prime}
is non-empty, there is a signature
\rho^{\prime}\in\vec{\tau^{\prime}}
such that
\sigma^{\prime}\leq_{\text{g}}\rho^{\prime}
. We can therefore apply axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{fg})}
. We get that for each signatures
\sigma^{(3)}
such that
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\sigma^{(3)}
,
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\sigma^{(3)}
and
\sigma^{(3)}(\wn_{\text{m}_{1}})
, which we can apply to
\sigma
and
\vec{\tau}
to get that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau}
and
\vec{\tau}(\wn_{\text{m}_{1}})
.

2.

Then, we prove by induction on the tree defined with the
\perp\!\!\!\perp
-relation and rooted by
S
that for each sequents
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
,
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
:

•

The initialisation is done with the first step.

•

For heredity, we have that there is a sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{(3)}}A^{(3)},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{(3)}}}\Delta^{(3)},\wn_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}({A^{\prime\prime}}^{\bot})
cut-connected to
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
on
\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime}
, meaning that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
, as the sequent is the conclusion of a promotion, we have that
\sigma^{\prime\prime}\leq_{s}\tau^{\prime\prime}
for a
s\in\{g,f,u\}
, we conclude using axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{gs})}
.

We conclude by induction and we use the inequalities from it to prove that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\rho}
.

∎

D.1.4
Justification for step
({\text{comm}}^{1}_{\oc_{\text{u}}})
: proof of
\Cref
ocureadinesscondition

We then cover the cases where we commute an
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-rule with the multi-cut.
The first case is where there are only a list of
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-rules in the hypotheses of the multi-cut:

Lemma 20

(Justification for step
({\text{comm}}^{1}_{\oc_{\text{u}}})
)
.

If

\pi

\vdash A,C

\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\tau

{\oc_{\text{u}}}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\tau}C

\mathcal{C}^{{\oc_{\text{u}}}}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\rho}B

is a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof, then

\pi

\vdash A,C

\mathcal{C}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash A,B

\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\rho

{\oc_{\text{u}}}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\rho}B

is a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof.

Proof.

We prove that for each sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}A^{\prime},\wn_{\tau^{\prime}}B^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{C}^{{\oc_{\text{u}}}}\cup\{\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\tau}B\}
, we have that
\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\tau^{\prime}
.

The
\perp\!\!\!\perp
-relation extended to sequent defines a tree on
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
. Taking
\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\tau}B
as the root, the ancestor relation of this tree is a well-founded relation. We can therefore do an induction proof:

•

The base case is given by the condition of application of
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
in the proof.

•

For heredity, we have that there is a sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}B^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\sigma^{\prime}}({A^{\prime}}^{\bot})
of
C^{\prime}
, connected on
\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}A^{\prime}
to our sequent.
By induction hypothesis, we have that
\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\sigma^{\prime}
.
The rule on top of
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}A^{\prime},\wn_{\tau^{\prime}}B^{\prime}
is an
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-promotion, we can use axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{trans})}
and with the application condition of the promotion, we get that
\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}f^{\prime}
.

We conclude by induction and get that
\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\rho
.
∎

D.1.5
Justification for step
({\text{comm}}^{2}_{\oc_{\text{u}}})
: proof of
\Cref
ocufreadinesscondition

The second case of
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-commutation is where we have an
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
-rule and where the hypotheses concluded by an
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-rule have empty contexts.

Lemma 21

(Justification for step
({\text{comm}}^{2}_{\oc_{\text{u}}})
)
.

Let

\pi

\vdash A,B

\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\tau

{\oc_{\text{u}}}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\tau}B

\mathcal{C}^{\oc}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma

be a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof with
\mathcal{C}
containing at least one proof concluded by an
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
-promotion ; and such that for each sequent conclusion of an
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-promotion has empty context. We have that

\pi

\vdash A,B

\mathcal{C}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash A,\Gamma

\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\rho}

{\oc_{\text{f}}}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma

is also a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof.

Proof.

If one
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
-rule has empty contexts, there is only one
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
,
\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma
is empty and therefore
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\rho}
is easily satisfied.
If not, we do our proof in two steps:

1.

As always, we notice that the
\perp\!\!\!\perp
-relation extended to sequent defines a tree on
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, meaning that there is a path in this tree, from
S:=\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\tau}B
to a sequent
S^{\prime}:=\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}A^{\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta
being the conclusion of an
{\oc_{\text{f}}}
-rule and with
\Delta
being non-empty. Without loss of generality, we ask for
S^{\prime}
to be the closest such sequent (with respect to the
\perp\!\!\!\perp
-relation).
We prove by induction on this path, starting from
S
and stopping one sequent before
S^{\prime}
, that for each sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}B^{\prime\prime}
, that
\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\tau^{\prime\prime}
:

•

The initialization comes from the condition of application of
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
on
S
.

•

The heredity comes from the condition of application of
{\oc_{\text{u}}}
on the sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}B^{\prime\prime}
and from lemma
(\mathsf{Ax_{trans})}
.

Finally, as
S^{\prime}
is linked by the cut-formula
\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}A^{\prime}
to one of these sequents, we get that
\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\sigma^{\prime}
. By the condition of application of
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
on
S^{\prime}
, we get that
\sigma^{\prime}\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\tau^{\prime}}
, and from lemma
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{us})}
, we have that
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\tau^{\prime}}
.

2.

We prove, for the remaining tree (which is rooted in
S^{\prime}
), that for each sequents
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
, that
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\tau^{\prime\prime}
. We prove it by induction.

•

Initialization was done at last point.

•

For heredity, if the sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is the conclusion of an
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-rule, by induction hypothesis, we get that
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
, and by
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
application condition we get that
\sigma^{\prime\prime}\leq_{\text{u}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
, we get
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
with axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{fu})}
.

•

For heredity, if the sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is the conclusion of an
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
-rule, by induction hypothesis, we get that
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
, and by
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
application condition we get that
\sigma^{\prime\prime}\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
, we get
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
with axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{trans})}
.

•

For heredity, if the sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is the conclusion of an
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-rule, then
\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is empty and the proposition is easily satisfied.

We conclude by induction and we use the inequalities from it to prove that
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\rho}
.
∎

D.1.6
Justification for step
({\text{comm}}^{3}_{\oc_{\text{u}}})
: proof of
\Cref
ocugreadinessconditionfcase

The following lemma deals with the case where there are sequents concluded by an
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-rule with non-empty context and where the first rule encountered is an
{\oc_{\text{f}}}
-rule.

Lemma 22

(Justification for step
({\text{comm}}^{3}_{\oc_{\text{u}}})
)
.

Let

\pi_{1}

\vdash A,B

\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\tau

{\oc_{\text{u}}}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\tau}B

\mathcal{C}^{{\oc_{\text{u}}}}_{1}

\pi_{2}

\vdash C,\Delta

\sigma^{\prime}\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\tau^{\prime}}

{\oc_{\text{f}}}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}C,\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta

\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{2}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma

be a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof, such that
\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{2}
contains a sequent conclusion of an
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
rule with non-empty context ;
\mathcal{C}:=\{\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\tau}B\}\cup\mathcal{C}^{{\oc_{\text{u}}}}_{1}\cup\{\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}C,\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta\}
are a cut-connected subset of sequents ; and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}:=\{\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}C,\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta\}\cup\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{2}
another one.
We have that

\pi_{1}

\vdash A,B

\mathcal{C}_{1}

\pi_{2}

\vdash C,\Delta

\vec{\tau^{\prime}}(\wn_{\text{m}_{1}})

\wn_{\text{m}_{1}}

\vdash C,\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta

\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{2}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash A,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma

\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\rho}

{\oc_{\text{g}}}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma

is also a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof.

Proof.

We do our proof in three steps:

1.

There is a sequent
S^{\prime\prime}:=\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
, with
\Delta^{\prime\prime}
being non-empty, which is conclusion of an
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-rule.
The
\perp\!\!\!\perp
-relation extended to sequents defines a tree on
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, therefore there is a path from the sequent
S^{\prime}:=\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}C,\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta
to the sequent
S^{\prime\prime}
, of sequents
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{(3)}}A^{(3)},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{(3)}}}\Delta^{(3)}
.
Let’s suppose without loss of generality, that this sequent is the closest such sequent to
S^{\prime}
.
We prove by induction on this path, starting from
S^{\prime}
and stopping one sequent before
S^{\prime\prime}
that
\sigma^{\prime}\leq_{\text{f}}\tau^{(3)}
:

•

The initialisation comes from the condition of application of
{\oc_{\text{f}}}
on
S^{\prime}
.

•

For the heredity, we have that the sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{(3)}}A^{(3)},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{(3)}}}\Delta^{(3)}
is cut-connected to a
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{(4)}}A^{(4)},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{(4)}}}\Delta^{(4)},\wn_{\sigma^{(3)}}({A^{(3)}}^{\perp})
on
\oc_{\sigma^{(3)}}A^{(3)}
, therefore
\sigma^{\prime}\leq_{\text{f}}\sigma^{(3)}
. We have two cases: either this sequent is the conclusion of an
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-rule and we apply axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{fu})}
, either of an
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
-rule and we apply axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{trans})}
. In each case, we have that
\sigma^{\prime}\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\tau^{(3)}}
.

We conclude by induction and get a sequent
S^{(3)}:=\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{(3)}}A^{(3)},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{(3)}}}\Delta^{(3)}
cut-connected to
S^{\prime\prime}
on the formula
\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime}
with
\sigma^{\prime}\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\tau^{(3)}}
. From that we get that
\sigma^{\prime}\leq_{\text{f}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
. Moreover, we have that
\sigma^{\prime\prime}\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
. As
\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is non-empty, there is a signature
\rho^{\prime\prime}\in\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
such that
\sigma^{\prime\prime}\leq_{\text{g}}\rho^{\prime\prime}
. We can therefore apply axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{fg})}
. We get that for each signatures
\sigma^{(4)}
such that
\sigma^{\prime}\leq_{\text{f}}\sigma^{(4)}
,
\sigma^{\prime}\leq_{\text{g}}\sigma^{(4)}
and
\sigma^{(4)}(\wn_{\text{m}_{1}})
, which we can apply to
\sigma^{\prime}
and
\vec{\tau^{\prime}}
to get that
\sigma^{\prime}\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime}}
and
\vec{\tau^{\prime}}(\wn_{\text{m}_{1}})
.

2.

Again, we notice that the
\perp\!\!\!\perp
-relation extended to sequent defines a tree on
\mathcal{C}
, meaning that there is a path in this tree, from
S:=\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\tau}B
to
S^{\prime}
.
We prove by induction on this path, starting from
S
and stopping one sequent before
S^{\prime}
, that for each sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{(3)}}A^{(3)},\wn_{\tau^{(3)}}B^{(3)}
, that
\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\tau^{(3)}
:

•

The initialization comes from the condition of application of
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
on
S
.

•

The heredity comes from the condition of application of
{\oc_{\text{u}}}
on the sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{(3)}}A^{(3)},\wn_{\tau^{(3)}}B^{(3)}
and from lemma
(\mathsf{Ax_{trans})}
.

Finally, as
S^{\prime}
is linked by the cut-formula
\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}A^{\prime}
to one of these sequents, we get that
\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\sigma^{\prime}
.

3.

Finally, we prove that for each sequents
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{(3)}}A^{(3)},\wn_{\tau^{(3)}}\Delta^{(3)}
of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
,
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\tau^{(3)}
. We prove it by induction as
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is a tree with the
\perp\!\!\!\perp
-relation.

•

Initialization comes from the face that
\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\sigma^{\prime}
,
\sigma^{\prime}\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime}}
and axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{us})}
.

•

For heredity, we have that there is a sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{(4)}}A^{(4)},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{(4)}}}\Delta^{(4)},\wn_{\sigma^{(3)}}(A^{(3)})^{\bot}
of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, connected on
\oc_{\sigma^{(3)}}A^{(3)}
to our sequent.
By induction hypothesis, we have that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\sigma^{(3)}
.
The rule on top of
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{(3)}}A^{(3)},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{(3)}}}\Delta^{(3)}
is a promotion. We have two cases:

–

If it’s a
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-promotion, we can use axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{trans})}
and with the application condition of the promotion, we get that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{(3)}}
.

–

If it’s an
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
-promotion or an
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-promotion, we can use axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{gs})}
and with the application condition of the promotion, we get that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{(3)}}
.

We conclude by induction.

We got two important properties:

1.

For each sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{(3)}}A^{(3)},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{(3)}}}\Delta^{(3)}
of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, we have that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{(3)}}
.

2.

We have
\vec{\tau^{\prime}}(\wn_{\text{m}_{1}})
.

We conclude using inequalities of the first property to find that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\rho}
. And we use the second property for the
(\wn_{\text{m}_{1}})
-rule.
∎

D.1.7
Justification for step
({\text{comm}}^{4}_{\oc_{\text{u}}})
: proof of
\Cref
ocugreadinessconditiongcase

The last lemma of promotion commutation is about the case where we commute an
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-promotion but when first meeting an
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-promotion.

Lemma 23

(Justification for step
({\text{comm}}^{4}_{\oc_{\text{u}}})
)
.

Let

\pi_{1}

\vdash A,B

\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\tau

{\oc_{\text{u}}}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\tau}B

\mathcal{C}^{{\oc_{\text{u}}}}_{1}

\pi_{2}

\vdash C,\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta

\sigma^{\prime}\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime}}

{\oc_{\text{g}}}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}C,\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta

\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{2}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma

be a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof such that
\mathcal{C}:=\{\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\tau}B\}\cup\mathcal{C}^{{\oc_{\text{u}}}}_{1}\cup\{\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}C,\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta\}
are a cut-connected subset of sequents ; and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}:=\{\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}C,\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta\}\cup\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{2}
another one. Then,

\pi_{1}

\vdash A,B

\mathcal{C}_{1}

\pi_{2}

\vdash C,\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta

\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{2}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash A,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma

\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\rho}

{\oc_{\text{g}}}

\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma

is also a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof.

Proof.

We do our proof in two steps:

1.

First, we prove that for each sequents
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A,\wn_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}B
of
\mathcal{C}\setminus\{\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}C,\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta\}
that
\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\tau^{\prime\prime}
. We prove it by induction on this list starting with the sequent
S:=\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\tau}}B
(it is a list with the
\perp\!\!\!\perp
-relation):

•

Initialization comes from the condition of application of
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
on
S
.

•

Heredity comes from the condition of application of
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
on the concerned sequent, from induction hypothesis and from axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{trans})}
.

We conclude by induction and deduce from the obtained property that
\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\sigma^{\prime}
.

2.

We then prove that for each sequents
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A,\wn_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}\Delta
of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
,
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
. We prove it by induction on
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
as the
\perp\!\!\!\perp
-relation defines a tree on it, for which we take
S^{\prime}:=\oc_{\sigma^{\prime}}C,\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta
as the root.

•

The initialization comes from
\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\sigma^{\prime}
that we showed for first step, from
\sigma^{\prime}\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime}}
which is the condition of application of
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
on
S^{\prime}
and from axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{us})}
.

•

For heredity, we have that there is a sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{(3)}}A^{(3)},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{(3)}}}\Delta^{(3)},\wn_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}({A^{\prime\prime}}^{\perp})
of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, connected on
\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime}
to our sequent.
By induction hypothesis, we have that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
.
The rule on top of
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is a promotion. We have two cases:

–

If it’s a
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-promotion, we can use axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{trans})}
and with the application condition of the promotion, we get that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
.

–

If it’s an
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
-promotion or an
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-promotion, we can use axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{gs})}
and with the application condition of the promotion, we get that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
.

We conclude by induction

From the inequalities that we get from induction, we can easily prove that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\rho}
.
∎

D.1.8
Justification for step
(\text{principal}_{\wn_{\text{c}}})
: proof of
\Cref
contrPrincipLemma

Then we have the principal cases, starting with the contraction:

)
.

If

is a

\mathsf{mcut(\iota^{\prime},\perp\!\!\!\perp^{\prime})}

\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \immediate;

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma^{\prime},\dots,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma^{\prime}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \immediate;
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

\bar{\vec{\rho}}(\wn_{\text{c}_{i}})

\wn_{\text{c}_{i}}^{\bar{\vec{\rho}}}

\vdash\Gamma,\wn_{\vec{\rho}}\Gamma^{\prime}

is also a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof.

Proof.

We prove for each sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{\wn_{\sigma}A}^{\oc}
, we have that
\sigma\leq_{s}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
(for one
s\in\{g,f,u\}
. As the relation
\perp\!\!\!\perp
defines a tree on
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{\wn_{\sigma}A}
(rooted on the sequent
S:=\vdash\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime}
which is the sequent connected to
\vdash\wn_{\sigma}A,\Delta
on
\wn_{\sigma}A
), we do a proof by induction on this tree:

•

Initialization comes from the application condition of the promotion.

•

For heredity, we get from induction hypothesis that
\sigma\leq_{s}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
for a
s\in\{g,f,u\}
, from the condition of application of the promotion, we get that
\sigma^{\prime\prime}\leq_{s^{\prime}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
(again for a
s^{\prime}\in\{g,f,u\}
), depending on the cases, from axioms
(\mathsf{Ax_{trans})}
,
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{gs})}
,
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{fu})}
,
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{fg})}
,
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{us})}
, we get that
\sigma\leq_{s^{\prime\prime}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
for a
s^{\prime\prime}\in\{g,f,u\}
.

We conclude by induction, we get using the obtained property, the fact that
\sigma(\wn_{\text{c}_{i}})
and from axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{c})}
, that for each sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{\wn_{\sigma}A}^{\oc}
,
\bar{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}(\wn_{\text{c}_{i}})
. We use property
1
to get that
\bar{\vec{\rho}}(\wn_{\text{c}_{i}})
is true, making the derivation valid in the proof of the statement.
∎

D.1.9
Justification for step
(\text{comm}_{\wn_{\text{m}}})
: proof of
\Cref
mpxPrincipLemma

Before justifying the case for the multiplexing principal reduction, we recall
\Cref
defi:mpxPrincipompx together
with a graphical representation to make it more understandable:

Definition 16

(
{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{O}}_{\text{mpx}}}_{S^{\oc}}(\mathcal{C}^{\oc})
contexts)
.

Let
\pi
be some
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof concluded in a
\text{mcut}(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)
inference,

\mathcal{C}^{\oc}
a context of the multicut which is a tree with respect to a cut-relation
\perp\!\!\!\perp
and

S^{\oc}
be a sequent of
\mathcal{C}^{\oc}
that we shall consider as the root of the tree.

We define a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-context
{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{O}}_{\text{mpx}}}_{S^{\oc}}(\mathcal{C}^{\oc})
altogether with two sets of sequents,
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{m}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}
and
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{c}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}
, by induction on the tree ordering on
\mathcal{C}^{\oc}
:

Let
\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{n}
be the sons of
S^{\oc}
, such that
\mathcal{C}^{\oc}=(S^{\oc},(\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{n}))
, we have two cases:

•

S^{\oc}=S^{\oc_{\text{g}}}
, then we define
{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{O}}_{\text{mpx}}}_{S^{\oc}}(\mathcal{C}^{\oc}):=(S,(\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{n}))
;
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{m}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}:=\emptyset
;
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{c}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}:=\mathcal{C}^{\oc}
.

•

S^{\oc}=S^{\oc_{\text{f}}}
or
S^{\oc}=S^{\oc_{\text{u}}}
, then let the root of
\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{i}
be
S_{i}^{\oc}
, we define
{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{O}}_{\text{mpx}}}_{S^{\oc}}(\mathcal{C}^{\oc})
as

(S,{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{O}}_{\text{mpx}}}_{S_{1}^{\oc}}(\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{1}),\dots,{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{O}}_{\text{mpx}}}_{S_{n}^{\oc}}(\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{n}))
,
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{m}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}
as
\{S^{\oc}\}\cup\bigcup\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{m}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{i},S_{i}^{\oc}}
and
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{c}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}
as
\bigcup\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{c}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{i},S_{i}^{\oc}}
.

Below is a graphical picture of the above definition in the second case (
S^{\oc}=S^{\oc_{\text{f}}}
or
S^{\oc}=S^{\oc_{\text{u}}}
) when all its sons (for the tree relation induced by
\perp\!\!\!\perp
) are of the form
S_{i}^{\oc_{\text{g}}}
(which illustrates both cases of the definition in one picture) :

\displaystyle S^{\oc}
\mathcal{C}^{{\oc_{\text{f}}}/{\oc_{\text{u}}}}
\displaystyle S_{1}^{{\oc_{\text{g}}}}
\displaystyle S_{n}^{{\oc_{\text{g}}}}
\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{1}
\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{n}
\displaystyle\vdots
\displaystyle\vdots
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{m}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{c}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}

\displaystyle S
\mathcal{C}
\displaystyle S_{1}
\displaystyle S_{n}
\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{1}
\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{n}
\displaystyle\vdots
\displaystyle\vdots

Finally, we have the multiplexing principal case:

Lemma 25

(Justification for step
(\text{comm}_{\wn_{\text{m}}})
)
.

Let

\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}

\vdash\overbrace{A,\dots,A}^{i},\Delta

\sigma(\wn_{\text{m}_{i}})

\wn_{\text{m}_{i}}

\vdash\wn_{\sigma}A,\Delta

\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{\wn_{\sigma}A}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash\Gamma,\wn_{\rho^{\prime}}\Gamma^{\prime},\wn_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}

be a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof with
\Gamma
being sent on
\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}\cup{\Delta}
by
\iota
;
\wn_{\vec{\rho^{\prime\prime}}}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
being sent on sequent of
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{m}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}
; and
\wn_{\vec{\rho^{\prime}}}\Gamma^{\prime}
being sent on
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{c}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}
, where
S^{\oc}:=\oc_{\sigma}A,\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime}
is the sequent cut-connected to
\vdash\wn_{\sigma}A,\Delta
on the formula
\wn_{\sigma}A
.
We have that

\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}

\vdash\overbrace{A,\dots,A}^{i},\Delta

\overbrace{{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{O}}_{\text{mpx}}}_{S^{\oc}}(\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{\wn_{\sigma}A})\hskip 8.19447pt\dots\hskip 8.19447pt{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{O}}_{\text{mpx}}}_{S^{\oc}}(\mathcal{C}^{\oc}_{\wn_{\sigma}A})}^{i}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota^{\prime},\perp\!\!\!\perp^{\prime})}

\vdash\Gamma,\overbrace{\Gamma^{\prime},\dots,\Gamma^{\prime}}^{i},\overbrace{\wn_{\vec{\rho^{\prime\prime}}}\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\dots,\wn_{\vec{\rho^{\prime\prime}}}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}^{i}

\hskip-42.67912pt\bar{\vec{\rho^{\prime}}}(\wn_{\text{m}_{i}})

\wn_{\text{m}_{i}}^{\bar{\vec{\rho^{\prime}}}}

\vdash\Gamma,\wn_{\vec{\rho^{\prime}}}\Gamma^{\prime},\overbrace{\wn_{\vec{\rho^{\prime\prime}}}\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\dots,\wn_{\vec{\rho^{\prime\prime}}}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}^{i}

\hskip-28.45274pt\bar{\vec{\rho^{\prime\prime}}}(\wn_{\text{c}_{i}})

\wn_{\text{c}_{i}}^{\bar{\vec{\rho^{\prime\prime}}}}

\vdash\Gamma,\wn_{\vec{\rho^{\prime}}}\Gamma^{\prime},\wn_{\vec{\rho^{\prime\prime}}}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}

is also a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof.

Proof.

We prove that for each sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
of
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{c}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}
,
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
and that for each sequent
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
of
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{m}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}
,
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
or
\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
. The
\perp\!\!\!\perp
-relation defines a tree rooted on
\lx@sectionsign^{\oc}
, we do a proof by induction:

•

If
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is in
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{m}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}
, then its antecedent is also in
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{m}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}
, by induction, we have the
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
or
\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
. Moreover, the promotion applied on
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{f^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is an
{\oc_{\text{f}}}
or an
{\oc_{\text{u}}}
promotion. We therefore have either by axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{us})}
, either by axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{trans})}
, either by axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{fu})}
, that
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
or
\sigma\leq_{\text{u}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
.

•

If
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is in
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{c}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}
, and that its antecedent is in
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{m}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}
, then by induction, we have that
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
or
\sigma\leq_{\text{f}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
. Moreover, the promotion applied on
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{f^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is an
{\oc_{\text{g}}}
promotion. Therefore, we have by axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{us})}
or
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{fg})}
that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
.

•

If
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A^{\prime\prime},\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is in
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{c}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}
, and that its antecedent is in
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{c}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{\oc}}
, then by induction, we have that
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
. Therefore, by axiom
(\mathsf{Ax_{\leq}^{gs})}
,
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
.

Finally we get that for all sequents
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A,\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
of
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{m}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{{}^{\prime}\oc}}
,
\bar{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}(\wn_{\text{m}_{i}})
are true, as
\sigma\leq_{s}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
,
\wn_{\text{m}_{i}}(\sigma)
(
s\in\{f,u\}
) and by lemma
(\mathsf{Ax^{fu}_{m})}
.
We also get that for all sequents
\vdash\oc_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}A,\wn_{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta
of
\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{c}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{{}^{\prime}\oc}}
,
\bar{\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}}(\wn_{\text{c}_{i}})
are true as
\sigma\leq_{\text{g}}\vec{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
,
\wn_{\text{c}_{i}}(\sigma)
and by lemma
(\mathsf{Ax^{g}_{m})}
.

From the condition on the proof of the statement and from property
1
, we get that
\bar{\vec{g^{\prime}}}(\wn_{\text{m}_{i}})
and
\bar{\vec{g^{\prime\prime}}}(\wn_{\text{c}_{i}})
are true and so that the right proof is correct.
∎

D.2
Rule permutations

Definition 17

(Permutation of rules)
.

We define
one-step rule permutation
on (pre-)proofs of
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
with rules of figure
12
.

Given a
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
(pre-)proof
\pi
and
p\in\{l,r,i\}^{*}
a path in the proof, we define
\text{perm}(\pi,p)
by induction on
p
:

•

the proof
\text{perm}(\pi,\epsilon)
is the proof obtained by applying the one-step rule permutation at the root of
\pi
if it is possible, either it is not defined;

•

we define
\text{perm}(q(\pi^{\prime}),i\cdot p^{\prime}):=r(\text{perm}(\pi^{\prime},q^{\prime}))
if
\text{perm}(\pi^{\prime},q^{\prime})
is defined, otherwise it is not defined;

•

we define
\text{perm}(q(\pi_{l},\pi_{r}),l\cdot q^{\prime}):=q(\text{perm}(\pi_{l},q^{\prime}),\pi_{r})
if
\text{perm}(\pi_{l},q^{\prime})
is defined, otherwise it is not defined;

•

we define
\text{perm}(q(\pi_{l},\pi_{r}),r\cdot q^{\prime}):=q(\text{perm}(\pi_{l},q^{\prime}),\pi_{r})
if
\text{perm}(\pi_{l},q^{\prime})
is defined, otherwise it is not defined;

•

for each other cases,
\text{perm}(\pi,p)
is not defined.

A
sequence of rule permutation
starting from a
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
pre-proof
\pi
is a (possibly empty) sequence
(p_{i})_{i\in\lambda}
(
\lambda\in\omega
), where
p_{i}\in\{l,r,i\}
such that if we set
\pi_{0}:=\pi
, then the sequence
(\pi_{i})_{i\in 1+\lambda}
defined by induction by
\pi_{i+1}:=\text{perm}(\pi_{i},p_{i})
are all defined. We say that the sequence
(\pi_{i})_{i\in 1+\lambda}
is the
sequence of proofs associated to the sequence of rule permutation
.
We say that the sequence
ends on

\pi_{\lambda}
if
\lambda
is finite, we also write it
\text{perm}(\pi,(p_{i})_{i\in\lambda})
.
