Figure 12:
One-step rule permutation

Lemma 26

(Robustness of the proof structure to rule permutation)
.

One-step rule permutation does not modify the structure of the proof.

Proof.

This lemma is immediate as the substitutions are defined between unary rule.
∎

Definition 18

(Finiteness of permutation of rules)
.

Let
\pi
be a
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
(pre-)proof, and let
(p_{i})_{i\in\lambda}
be a sequence of rule permutation starting from
\pi
and let
(\pi_{i})_{i\in 1+\lambda}
be the sequence of proofs associated to it, let
q\in\{l,r,i\}^{*}
be a path to the conclusion sequent of a rule
(r)
of
\pi
, we define the
sequence of residuals

(q_{i})_{i\in 1+\lambda}
of
(r)
in
\pi_{i}
to be a sequence of path defined by induction on
i
:

•

if
i=0
,
q_{0}=q
;

•

if
p_{i}=q_{i}
, then
q_{i+1}:=q_{i}\cdot i
.

•

if
q_{i}=p_{i}\cdot i
then
q_{i+1}:=p_{i}
.

•

else
q_{i+1}:=q_{i}
.

We say that a rule
(r)
in
\pi
is
finitely permuted
if its sequence of residuals is ultimately constant.
We say that
(p_{i})_{i\in\lambda}
is a
rule permutation sequence with finite permutation of rules
if each rule of
\pi_{0}
is finitely permuted.

Proposition 3

(Convergence of permutation with finite permutation of rules)
.

Let
\pi
be a
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
pre-proof and let
(p_{i})_{i\in\omega}
be a permutation sequence with finite permutation of rules starting from
\pi
, then the sequence is converging.

Proof.

Let
(\pi_{i})_{i\in\omega}
be the sequence of proofs associated to the sequence.
Let’s suppose for the sake of contradiction that the sequence is not converging. It implies, using lemma
26
, that there is an infinite sequence of strictly increasing indexes
\varphi(i)
such that the
(r_{\varphi(i)})
are all at the same position.
This implies by finiteness of permutation of one rules, than there are an infinite number of rules of
\pi_{0}
which have
(r_{\varphi(i)})
in their residuals, implying that one of the rules below the position of
(r_{\varphi(i)})
in
\pi_{0}
has infinitely many residuals being equal to
(r_{i})
or below
(r_{i})
contradicting the finitess of permutation of one rule hypothesis.
∎

Proposition 4

(Preservation of validity for permutations with finite permutation of rules)
.

Let
\pi
be a
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
pre-proof and let
(p_{i})_{i\in\omega}
be a permutation sequence with finite permutation of rules starting from
\pi
and converging (thanks to lemma
3
to a pre-proof
\pi^{\prime}
. Then
\pi
is valid if and only if
\pi^{\prime}
is.

Proof.

From lemma
26
, we have that the structure of the trees of the sequence stays the same, therefore the structure of
\pi
is the same than the structure of
\pi^{\prime}
, moreover the threads of
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
are the same if we remove indexes where the thread is not active.
Therefore validity is easily preserved both ways.
∎

D.3
Details on
\Cref
redSeqTranslationFiniteness

Lemma 27
.

Let
\pi_{0}
be a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
proof and let
\pi_{0}\rightsquigarrow\pi_{1}
be a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
step of reduction. There exist a finite number of
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
proofs
\theta_{0},\dots,\theta_{n}
such that
\theta_{0}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow\theta_{n},\quad\pi_{0}^{\circ}=\theta_{0}
and
\theta_{n}=\pi_{1}^{\circ}
up to a finite number of rule permutations, done only on rules that just permuted down the
(\text{mcut})
.

To prove this lemma, we need the following one.
This lemma prove that when starting from the translation of a proof containing derelictions promotions and functorial promotions, there exist an order of execution of cut-elimination step that will make them disappear or commute under the cut.
This order depends on how the proof is translated, for instance the following (opened) proof:

\vdash A,B,C

{\oc_{\text{f}}}

\vdash\oc A,\wn B,\wn C

\vdash C^{\perp}

{\oc_{\text{f}}}

\vdash\oc C^{\perp}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash\oc A,\wn B

has two translations:

\vdash A,B,C

\wn_{\text{d}}

\vdash A,B,\wn C

\wn_{\text{d}}

\vdash A,\wn B,\wn C

\oc_{\text{p}}

\vdash\oc A,\wn B,\wn C

\vdash C^{\perp}

\oc_{\text{p}}

\vdash\oc C^{\perp}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash\oc A,\wn B

\vdash A,B,C

\wn_{\text{d}}

\vdash A,\wn B,C

\wn_{\text{d}}

\vdash A,\wn B,\wn C

\oc_{\text{p}}

\vdash\oc A,\wn B,\wn C

\vdash C^{\perp}

\oc_{\text{p}}

\vdash\oc C^{\perp}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash\oc A,\wn B

To eliminate cuts, we apply in both the same cut-elimination steps but in a different order. We apply in both an
(\oc_{\text{p}})
commutative step, then apply in the first one a dereliction commutative step and a
(\oc_{\text{p}})/(\wn_{\text{d}})
principal case; whereas in the second one we first apply the
(\oc_{\text{p}})/(\wn_{\text{d}})
principal case then the dereliction commutative step.

Lemma 28
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
d_{1},\dots,d_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
and let
p_{1},\dots,p_{n}\in\{0,1\}
. Let
\pi
be a
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
-proof concluded by an (
mcut
)-rule, on top of which there is a list of
n
proofs
\pi_{1},\dots,\pi_{n}
. We ask for each
\pi_{i}
to be of one of the following forms depending on
p_{i}
:

•

If
p_{i}=1
, the
d_{i}+1
last rules of
\pi_{i}
are
d_{i}
derelictions and then a promotion rule. We ask for the principal formula of this promotion to be either a formula of the conclusion, or to be cut with a formula being principal in a proof
\pi_{j}
on one of the last
d_{j}+p_{j}
rules.

•

If
p_{i}=0
, the
d_{i}
last rules of
\pi_{i}
are
d_{i}
derelictions.

In each of these two cases, we ask for
\pi_{i}
that each principal formulas of the
d_{i}
derelictions to be either a formula of the conclusion of the multicut, either a cut-formula being cut with a formula appearing in
\pi_{j}
such that
p_{j}=1
.
We prove that
\pi
reduces through a finite number of
mcut
-reductions to a proof where each of the last
d_{i}+p_{i}
rules either were eliminated by a
(\oc_{\text{p}}/\wn_{\text{d}})
-principal case, or were commuted below the cut.

Proof.

We prove the property by induction on the sum of all the
d_{i}
and of all the
p_{i}
:

•

(Initialization). As the sum of the
d_{i}
and
p_{i}
is
0
, all
d_{i}
and
p_{i}
are equal to
0
, meaning that our statement is vacuously true.

•

(Heredity). We have several cases:

–

If the last rule of a proof
\pi_{i}
is a promotion or a dereliction for which the principal formula is in the conclusion of the (
mcut
), we do a commutation step on this rule obtaining
\pi^{\prime}
. We apply our induction hypothesis on the proof ending with the (
mcut
); and with parameters
d^{\prime}_{1},\dots,d^{\prime}_{n}
as well as
p^{\prime}_{1},\dots,p^{\prime}_{n}
and proofs
\pi^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\pi^{\prime}_{n}
. To describe these parameters we have two cases:

*

If the rule is a promotion. We take for each
j\in\llbracket 1,n\rrbracket
,
d^{\prime}_{j}=d_{j}
;
p^{\prime}_{j}=p_{j}
if
j\neq i
,
p^{\prime}_{i}=0
;
\pi^{\prime}_{j}=\pi_{j}
if
j\neq i
.

*

If the rule is a dereliction. We take for each
j\in\llbracket 1,n\rrbracket
,
d^{\prime}_{j}=d_{j}
if
j\neq i
,
d^{\prime}_{i}=d_{i}-1
;
p^{\prime}_{j}=p_{j}
.

The
\pi^{\prime}_{j}
will be the hypotheses of the (
mcut
) of
\pi^{\prime\prime}
. Note that
\sum d^{\prime}_{j}+\sum p^{\prime}_{j}=\sum d_{j}+\sum p_{j}-1
meaning that we can apply our induction hypothesis.
Combining our reduction step with the reduction steps of the induction hypothesis, we obtain the desired result.

–

If there are no rules from the conclusion but that one
\pi_{i}
ends with
d_{i}>0
and
p_{i}=0
, meaning that the proof ends by a dereliction on a formula
\wn F
. This means that there is proof
\pi_{j}
such that
p_{j}=1
and such that
\wn F
is cut with one of the formula of
\pi_{j}
, namely
\oc F^{\perp}
. As there are only one
\oc
-formula, and as
p_{j}=1
,
\oc F^{\perp}
is the principal rule of the last rule applied on
\pi_{j}
. We therefore can perform an
(\oc_{\text{p}}/\wn_{\text{d}})
principal case on the last rules from
\pi_{i}
and
\pi_{j}
, leaving us with a proof
\pi^{\prime}
with an (
mcut
) as conclusion. We apply the induction hypothesis on this proof with parameters
d^{\prime}_{1}=d_{1},\dots d^{\prime}_{i}=d^{\prime}_{i}-1\dots,d^{\prime}_{n}=d^{\prime}_{n}
,
p^{\prime}_{1}=p_{1},\dots,p^{\prime}_{j}=p^{\prime}_{j}-1,\dots,p^{\prime}_{n}=p_{n}
and with the proofs being the hypotheses of the multicut.
Combining our steps with the steps from the induction hypotheses, we obtain the desired result.

–

We will show that the case where there are no rules from the conclusion and that no
\pi_{i}
are such that
d_{i}>0
and
p_{i}=0
, is impossible. Supposing, for the sake of contradiction, that this case is possible. We will construct an infinite sequence of proofs
(\theta_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
all different and all being hypotheses of the multi-cut, which is impossible. We know that there exist a proof
\theta_{0}:=\pi_{j}
ending with a promotion on a formula
\oc A
and that this formula is not a formula from the conclusion. This proof is in relation by the
\perp\!\!\!\perp
-relation to another proof
\theta_{1}:=\pi_{j^{\prime}}
. We know that this proof cannot be
\pi_{j}
because the
\perp\!\!\!\perp
-relation extended to sequents is acyclic. This proof also ends with a promotion on a principal formula which is not from the conclusion. By repeating this process, we obtain the desired sequence
(\theta_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
, giving us a contradiction.

The statement is therefore true by induction
∎

Proof of lemma
11
.

Reductions from the non-exponential part of
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
translates easily to one step of reduction in
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
.
To prove the result on exponential part, we will describe each translation of the reductions of figure
5
and
7
.
For the commutative steps no commutation of rules are necessary.

•

Step
({\text{comm}}_{\oc_{\text{g}}})
. This step translates to the commutation of one
(\oc)
-rule in
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
, which is one step of reduction.

•

Step
({\text{comm}}^{1}_{\oc_{\text{f}}})
. We prove that lemma
28
applies to step
({\text{comm}}^{1}_{\oc_{\text{f}}})
. Taking the left proof from step
({\text{comm}}^{1}_{\oc_{\text{f}}})
and translating it in
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
, we obtain a proof:

\pi_{1}^{\circ}

\vdash A_{1}^{\circ},\Delta_{1}^{\circ}

\wn_{\text{d}}

\vdash A_{1}^{\circ},\wn\Delta_{1}^{\circ}

\oc_{\text{p}}

\vdash\oc A_{1}^{\circ},\wn\Delta_{1}^{\circ}

\dots

\pi_{n}^{\circ}

\vdash A_{n}^{\circ},\Delta_{n}^{\circ}

\wn_{\text{d}}

\vdash A_{n}^{\circ},\wn\Delta_{n}^{\circ}

\oc_{\text{p}}

\vdash\oc A_{n}^{\circ},\wn\Delta_{n}^{\circ}

\mathsf{mcut(\iota,\perp\!\!\!\perp)}

\vdash\oc A^{\circ},\wn\Gamma^{\circ}

with
\iota(1)=(i,1)
for some
i
and
n=1+\#(\mathcal{C})
.
We apply our result on this proof with all the
p_{i}
being equal to
1
and with
d_{i}=\#(\Delta_{i})
.
Moreover, we notice that there will be only one promotion rule commuting under the cut and that it commutes before any dereliction, giving us
the
translation of the functorial promotion under the multicut.

•

Step
({\text{comm}}^{2}_{\oc_{\text{f}}})
. As for
({\text{comm}}_{\oc_{\text{g}}})
, this step only translates to the commutation of one
(\oc)
-rule in
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
, which is one step of reduction.

•

Step
({\text{comm}}^{1}_{\oc_{\text{u}}})
.
This step translates to the commutation of one
(\oc_{\text{p}})
-rule, followed by
\#(\mathcal{C}^{{\oc_{\text{u}}}})

(\oc/\wn_{\text{d}})
principal steps and finally one
(\wn_{\text{d}})
commutation giving us the translation of a unary promotion under the multicut.

•

Step
({\text{comm}}^{2}_{\oc_{\text{u}}})
.
We prove this step using lemma
28
as for step
({\text{comm}}^{1}_{\oc_{\text{f}}})
.

•

Step
({\text{comm}}^{3}_{\oc_{\text{u}}})
and
({\text{comm}}^{4}_{\oc_{\text{u}}})
. Both of these steps translate to the commutation of one
(\oc_{\text{p}})
, followed by
\#(\mathcal{C}^{{\oc_{\text{u}}}}_{1})+1

(\oc/\wn_{\text{d}})
principal steps.

•

Step
(\text{comm}_{\wn_{\text{m}}})
. We must distinguish three cases based on
i
:

–

i=0
. This step translate to one
(\wn_{\text{w}})
-commutative step.

–

i=1
. This step translate to one
(\wn_{\text{d}})
-commutative step.

–

i>1
. This step translates to
i-1
commutation of
(\wn_{\text{c}})
and
i
commutation of
(\wn_{\text{d}})
.

•

Step
(\text{comm}_{\wn_{\text{c}}})
. This step translates to
i-1
commutation of
(\wn_{\text{c}})
.

•

Step
(\text{principal}_{\wn_{\text{c}}})
. This step translates to
i-1
contraction principal cases. At the end we obtain the following derivation under the multi-cut:

\vdash\Gamma^{\circ},\overbrace{\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ},\dots,\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}}^{i}

\wn_{\text{c}}

\vdash\Gamma^{\circ},\overbrace{\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ},\dots,\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}}^{i-1}

\vdots

\vdash\Gamma^{\circ},\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ},\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}

\wn_{\text{c}}

\vdash\Gamma^{\circ},\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}

which we can re-arrange to get the translation of
\#\Gamma^{\prime}

\wn_{\text{c}_{i}}^{\bar{\vec{\rho}}}
rules on each formulas of
\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}
.
Note that for
i=2
no rule permutation are needed.

•

Step
(\text{principal}_{\wn_{\text{m}}})
. If
i\geq 1
, this step translates in two phases:

1.

First
i-1
contraction principal cases;

2.

followed by
\#(\mathcal{S}^{\wn_{\text{m}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\oc},S^{{}^{\prime}\oc}})

(\wn_{\text{d}}/\oc)
-principal cases, and
\#(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
dereliction commutative cases.

To prove the second phase we re-use lemma
28
as for steps
({\text{comm}}^{2}_{\oc_{\text{u}}})
and
({\text{comm}}^{1}_{\oc_{\text{f}}})
.

Finally, the obtained proof under the multi-cut look like this:

\vdash\Gamma^{\circ},\overbrace{\wn{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ},\dots,\wn{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}}^{i},\overbrace{{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ},\dots,{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}}^{i}

\wn_{\text{d}}

\vdash\Gamma^{\circ},\overbrace{\wn{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ},\dots,\wn{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}}^{i},\overbrace{{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ},\dots,{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}}^{i-1},\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}

\vdots

\vdash\Gamma^{\circ},\overbrace{\wn{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ},\dots,\wn{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}}^{i},{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ},\overbrace{\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ},\dots,\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}}^{i-1}

\wn_{\text{d}}

\vdash\Gamma^{\circ},\overbrace{\wn{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ},\dots,\wn{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}}^{i},\overbrace{\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ},\dots,\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}}^{i}

\wn_{\text{c}}

\vdash\Gamma^{\circ},\overbrace{\wn{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ},\dots,\wn{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}}^{i-1},\overbrace{\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ},\dots,\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}}^{i-1}

\vdots

\vdash\Gamma^{\circ},\wn{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ},\wn{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ},\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ},\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}

\wn_{\text{c}}

\vdash\Gamma^{\circ},\wn{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ},\wn{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\circ}

which we can re-arrange to get the translation of
\#\Gamma^{\prime}

\wn_{\text{m}_{i}}^{\bar{\vec{\rho^{\prime\prime}}}}
, followed by the translation of
\#\Gamma^{\prime\prime}

\wn_{\text{c}_{i}}^{\bar{\vec{\rho^{\prime}}}}
.

If
i=0
, this step translates to a weakening principal case, giving us the translation of
\#\Gamma^{\prime}

\wn_{\text{m}_{0}}^{\bar{\vec{\rho^{\prime\prime}}}}
and
\#\Gamma^{\prime\prime}

\wn_{\text{c}_{0}}^{\bar{\vec{\rho^{\prime}}}}
with no commutation of rules necessary.
∎

D.4
Details on
\Cref
mcutonestepCompleteness

Lemma 29

(Completeness of the (
mcut
)-reduction system)
.

If there is a
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
-redex
\mathcal{R}
sending
\pi^{\circ}
to
{\pi^{\prime}}^{\circ}
then there exists a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-redex
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
sending
\pi
to a proof
\pi^{\prime\prime}
, such that in the translation of
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{R}
is applied.

Proof.

We only prove the exponential cases, the non-exponential cases being immediate. We have several cases:

•

If the case is the commutative step of a contraction or a dereliction or weakening
(r)
, as it is on top of a (
mcut
), it necessarily means that
(r)
comes from the translation of a multiplexing or a contraction rule
(r^{\prime})
which is also on top of an (
mcut
) in
\pi
, we can take
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
as the step commutating
(r^{\prime})
under the cut.

•

If it is a principal case again, we have that there is a contraction or a dereliction or weakening rule
(r)
on top of a (
mcut
) on a formula
\wn A
. It also means that each proofs cut-connected to
\wn A
ends with a promotion. As
\pi^{\circ}
is the translation of a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof, it means that
(r)
is contained in the translation of a multiplexing or contraction rule
(r^{\prime})
on a formula
\wn_{\sigma}A
on top of a (
mcut
). It also means that all the proofs cut-connected for this (
mcut
) to
\wn_{\sigma}A
are translations of promotions (no other rules than a promotion in
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}{}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
translates to a derivation ending with a promotion). Therefore the principal case on
(r^{\prime})
is possible, we can take
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
as it.

•

If it is the commutative step of a promotion
(r)
, it means that all the proofs of the contexts of the (
mcut
) are promotions.
Meaning that
(r)
is contained in the translation of a promotion
(r^{\prime})
on top of (
mcut
).
We also have that the context of this (
mcut
) are only proof ending with a promotion for the same reasons that last point.
We therefore need to make sure that each (
mcut
) with a context full of promotions are covered by the
\rightsquigarrow
-relation. Looking back at figure
5
together with conditions given by each corresponding lemmas, we have that:

–

Each
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-commutation is covered by the first case.

–

Each
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
-commutation is covered by the two cases that follows: the second of the two covers the case where there is an
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-promotion in hypotheses of the multicut with non-empty context, whereas the first one covers the case where there are no such
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-promotions in the hypotheses.

–

The
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-commutation is covered by all the remaining cases:

*

The first one covers
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-commutation when the hypotheses are all concluded by an
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-rule.

*

({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-commutation with
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
-rules and (possibly)
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-rule with empty context are covered by the second case.

*

({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-commutation with
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
-rules and
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
-rule with non-empty contexts is covered by the third and the fourth cases: the third case covering all the cases where the chain of
({\oc_{\text{u}}})
encounters a
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
first, the fourth one when it encounter a
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
first.

*

({\oc_{\text{u}}})
-commutation without
({\oc_{\text{f}}})
rules but with
({\oc_{\text{g}}})
with or without empty contexts is covered by last case.

∎

D.5
Details on the translation of fair reduction sequences

Corollary 5
.

For every fair
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
reduction sequences
(\pi_{i})_{i\in\omega}
, there exists:

•

a fair
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
reduction sequence
(\theta_{i})_{i\in\omega}
;

•

a sequence of strictly increasing
(\varphi(i))_{i\in\omega}
natural numbers;

•

for each
i
, an integer
k_{i}
and a finite sequence of rule permutations
(p_{i}^{k})_{k\in\llbracket 0,k_{i}-1\rrbracket}
starting from
\pi_{i}^{\circ}
and ending
\theta_{\varphi(i)}
. For convenience in the proof, let’s denote by
(\pi_{i}^{k})_{k\in\llbracket 0,k_{i}\rrbracket}
be the sequence of proofs associated to the permutation;

•

for all
i>i^{\prime}
,
p_{i}^{k}>p_{i}^{k^{\prime}}
if
k^{\prime}\in\llbracket 0,k_{i^{\prime}}-1
and
k\geq k_{i^{\prime}}
;

•

for all
i,k
,
p_{i}^{k}
are positions lower than the multicuts in
\pi_{i}^{\circ}
.

•

for each
i^{\prime}\geq i
and for each
k\in\llbracket 0,k_{i}-1\rrbracket,p^{k}_{i^{\prime}}=p^{k}_{i}

Proof.

We construct the sequence by induction on the steps of reductions of
(\pi_{i})_{i\in\omega}
.

•

For
i=0
: we take
\theta_{0}=\pi_{0}^{\circ}
,
\varphi(0)=0
and
k_{0}=0
.

•

For
i+1
, suppose we constructed everything up to rank
i
. We use lemma
11
on the step
\pi_{i}\to\pi_{i+1}

and get a finite sequence of reduction
\theta^{\prime}_{0}\to\dots\to\theta^{\prime}_{n}
, such that there is a permutation of rules
(p_{1},\dots,p_{m})
(
m\in\mathbb{N}
) starting on

\pi_{i+1}^{\circ}
and ending on
\theta^{\prime}_{n}
such that
p_{1},\dots,p_{m}
are at the depths of rules that just commuted down the multicut during the sequence
\theta^{\prime}_{0}\to\dots\to\theta^{\prime}_{n}
.
We have that
\theta^{\prime}_{0}=\pi_{i}^{\circ}
, therefore
(p_{i}^{0},\dots,p_{i}^{k_{i}-1})
is a sequence of reduction starting from
\theta^{\prime}_{0}
and ending on
\theta_{\varphi(i)}
.
As
{\theta^{\prime}}_{0}
and
\theta^{\prime}_{j}
are equal under the multicut rules of
\theta^{\prime}_{0}
(for each
j\in\llbracket 0,n\rrbracket
) and that depths
p_{i}^{j},j\in\llbracket 0,k_{i}-1\rrbracket
are under the multicuts of
\pi_{i}

, we have that
(p_{i}^{0},\dots,p_{i}^{k_{i}-1})
is a sequence of rule permutation starting on proof
\theta^{\prime}_{j}
.
Let’s denote by
{\theta^{\prime}}_{j}^{0},\dots,{\theta^{\prime}}_{j}^{k_{i}}
the sequence of proof associated to it.
We have that for the same reason,
{\theta^{\prime}}_{j}
is equal to
{\theta^{\prime}}_{j}^{k_{i}}
on top of the depths of multicuts of
\theta^{\prime}_{j}
. We therefore have that
{\theta^{\prime}}_{0}^{k_{i}},\dots,{\theta^{\prime}}_{n}^{k_{i}}
is an (
mcut
) reduction sequence of
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
starting from
\theta_{\varphi(i)}
.
As the two sequences of reductions
p_{1},\dots,p_{m}
and
p_{i}^{0},\dots,p_{i}^{k_{i}-1}
have disjoint sets of rules with non-empty traces,
we have that
p_{i}^{0},\dots,p_{i}^{k_{i}-1},p_{1},\dots,p_{m}
is a sequence of rule permutation starting from
\pi^{\prime}_{i+1}
and ending on the same proof than the proof ending the sequence
p_{1},\dots,p_{m},p_{i}^{0},\dots,p_{i}^{k_{i}-1}
, namely
{\theta^{\prime}}_{n}^{k_{i}}
.
By setting
\varphi(i+1):=\varphi(i)+n,\quad\theta_{\varphi(i)+j}:={\theta^{\prime}}_{j}^{k_{i}}
(for
j\in\llbracket 0,n\rrbracket
),
p^{j}_{i+1}=p^{j}_{i}
for
j\leq k_{i}-1
and
p^{k_{i}-1+j}_{i+1}=p_{j}
for
j\in\llbracket 1,m\rrbracket
, we have our property.

Here is a summary of the objects used in the inductive step:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{i+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{i+1}^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{i}^{\circ}=\theta^{\prime}_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta^{\prime}_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta^{\prime}_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\theta^{\prime}}^{1}_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\theta^{\prime}}^{1}_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\theta^{\prime}}^{1}_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\theta^{\prime}}_{0}^{k_{i}}=\theta_{\varphi(i)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\theta^{\prime}}_{j}^{k_{i}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{\varphi(i+1)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{i}^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1},\dots,p_{m}}

We get fairness of
(\theta_{i})_{i\in\omega}
from lemma
12
and from the fact that after the translation of an (
mcut
)-step,
\pi^{\circ}\rightsquigarrow{\pi^{\prime}}^{\circ}
, each residual of a redex
\mathcal{R}
of
\pi^{\circ}
, is contained in the translations of residuals of the associated redex
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
of lemma
12
.
∎

D.6
Details on the main theorem

Theorem 5
.

Every fair (
mcut
)-reduction sequence of
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
converges to a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
cut-free proof.

Proof.

Consider a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
fair reduction sequence
(\pi_{i})_{i\in 1+\lambda}
(
\lambda\in\omega+1
).
If the sequence is finite, we use lemma
11
and we are done.
If the sequence is infinite, using corollary
1
we get a fair infinite
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
reduction sequence
(\theta_{i})_{i\in\omega}
and a sequence
(\varphi(i))_{i\in\omega}
of natural numbers.
By theorem
1
, we know that
(\theta_{i})_{i\in\omega}
converges to a cut-free proof
\theta
of
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
.
We now prove that the sequence
(\pi_{i})_{i\in\omega}
converges to a
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
pre-proof
\pi
such that
\pi^{\circ}=\theta
up to a permutation of rules (the permutations of one particular rule being finite).

First, we prove that for each depth
d
, there is an
i
such that there are no
(\text{mcut})
-rules under depth
d
in
\pi_{i}
. Suppose for the sake of contradiction that there exist a depth
d
such that there always exist a
(\text{mcut})
at depth
d
.
There is a rank
i^{\prime}
and an
(\text{mcut})
rule in
\pi_{i^{\prime}}
such that for each
i\geq i^{\prime}
,
\pi_{i}
will always contain this
(\text{mcut})
and (therefore) the branch
b
to it never changes.
The translations
\pi_{i^{\prime}}^{\circ}
contains the translation of the branch
b
which also ends with an
mcut
.
Since
\pi_{i^{\prime}}^{\circ}
is equal to
\theta_{\varphi(i^{\prime})}
up to the permutations of rules under the multicut and that these permutations do not change the depths of the
(\text{mcut})
rules, we have that the
\theta_{\varphi(i)}
all contains a
(\text{mcut})
at a depth equal to the depth of the translation of
b
.
This contradicts the productivity of this sequence of reduction, we therefore have that
(\pi_{i})
converges to a pre-proof
\pi
.

Second, we prove that
\pi^{\circ}
is equal to
\theta
up to a permutation of rules (the permutations of one particular rule being finite).
The condition on the sequence given by corollary
1
defines a sequence of rule permutation starting from
\pi^{\circ}
:

p_{0}^{0},\dots,p_{0}^{k_{0}-1},p_{1}^{k_{0}},\dots,p_{1}^{k_{1}-1},\dots,p_{n}^{k_{n-1}},\dots,p_{n+1}^{k_{n}},\dots,

moreover we have that this is a permutation of rules with finite permutation, therefore this sequence of rule permutation converges to a
\mu\text{LL}^{\infty}
pre-proof
\pi^{\prime}
.
We have for each
i
, that the end of the sequence of rule permutation

p_{0}^{0},\dots,p_{0}^{k_{0}-1},p_{1}^{k_{0}},\dots,p_{1}^{k_{1}-1},\dots,p_{i}^{k_{i-1}},\dots,p_{i}^{k_{i}-1}

starting from
\pi^{\circ}
is equal to
\pi_{i}^{k_{i}}
under the multicuts. Therefore we have that the sequence
(\pi_{i}^{k_{i}})_{i\in\omega}=(\theta_{\varphi(i)})_{i\in\omega}
converges to
\pi^{\prime}
and therefore that
\pi^{\prime}=\theta
.
As rule permutation with finite permutation and
(-)^{\circ}
translation are robust to validity (both ways), we have that
\pi
is valid.
∎

D.7
Details on corollary
2

Corollary 6

(Cut Elimination for
superLL
)
.

Cut elimination holds for
\textnormal{{$\text{superLL}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
as soon as the 8 cut-elimination axioms of definition
1
are satisfied.

Proof.

Any
\textnormal{{$\text{superLL}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof is also
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-proof therefore any sequence of (
mcut
)-reductions converges to a cut-free proof. A cut-free proof of sequents containing only
\textnormal{{$\text{superLL}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
-formulas and valid rules from
\textnormal{{$\mu\text{superLL}^{\infty}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
is necessarily a
\textnormal{{$\text{superLL}$}}(\mathcal{E},\leq_{\text{g}},\leq_{\text{f}},\leq_{\text{u}})
(cut-free) proof.
∎
